, neither will affect any further
semantic description, since we will consider only a finite number of
semantic steps: only a finite number of messages will have been sent
after each semantic step of the system.

For simplicity and to stay closer to the intuition, throughout the
paper we will stick to the presentation of trees as a single
codatatype (Definition
A.4
). Where we need to
perform induction on prefixes we will explicitly mention it.

A.2.
Local Types

For
local types
(or

local session types
), we take the same approach as global
types: we formalise their inductive syntax and then we coinductively
unravel recursion to obtain possibly infinite trees.

Definition A.9 (Local types).

Local types
(datatype
l_ty
in
Local/Syntax.v
), ranged over by
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, are generated by the following grammar:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}&::=&\\
&&\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\mathbin{\mathbf{|\!\!|}}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\mathbin{\mathbf{|\!\!|}}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\mathbin{\mathbf{|\!\!|}}\\
&&\;\mathbin{\mathbf{|\!\!|}}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{![{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\mathbin{\mathbf{|\!\!|}}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\end{array}

We require that
I\neq\emptyset,
and
\ell_{i}\neq\ell_{j}
whenever
i\neq j,
for all
i,j\in I
.

The session type
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
says that no further communication is
possible and the protocol is completed. Recursion is modelled by the session type
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. The

send type

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{![{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
says that the participant implementing the type must choose a labelled message to send to
{\sf q}
; if the participant chooses the message
\ell_{i}
, for some
i\in I,
it must include in the message to
{\sf q}
a payload value of sort

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{i}
, and continue as prescribed by
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. The
receive
type

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
requires to wait to receive a value of sort
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{i}
(for some
i\in I
) from the participant
{\sf p}
, via a message with label
\ell_{i}
; if the received message has label
\ell_{i}
, the protocol will continue as prescribed by
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

We restrict ourselves to closed local types and we require recursion
to be guarded. In the text we will mostly implicitly assume those. We
define analogous predicates to the ones for global types.

Definition A.10 (Guardedness for local types).

We say that a local type is
guarded
(
lguarded
in

Local/Syntax.v
) according to the following definition:

\small\begin{array}[]{c}\dfrac{}{\texttt{guarded}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\hskip 9.24994pt\dfrac{}{\texttt{guarded}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\hskip 9.24994pt\dfrac{\texttt{not\_pure\_rec}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{guarded}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\texttt{guarded}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\\[11.38109pt]
\dfrac{\forall i\in I.\texttt{guarded}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\texttt{guarded}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{![{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\hskip 9.24994pt\dfrac{\forall i\in I.\texttt{guarded}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\texttt{guarded}\ (\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}\end{array}

where
\texttt{not\_pure\_rec}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
means that
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is
different from
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{Y_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\dots\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{Y_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
also
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\neq\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Definition A.11 (Free variables and closure (local types)).

The set of
free variables
of a local type
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\texttt{fv}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, (
l_fidx
in
Local/Syntax.v
) is defined as follows.

\small\begin{array}[]{c}\texttt{fv}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=\emptyset\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{fv}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=\{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{fv}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=(\texttt{fv}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}))\backslash\{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\}\\
\texttt{fv}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{![{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}\texttt{fv}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\\
\texttt{fv}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=\bigcup\limits_{i\in I}\texttt{fv}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\end{array}

We say that
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is
closed
(
l_closed
in

Local/Syntax.v
) if it does not contain free variables, namely

\texttt{closed}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leftrightarrow(\texttt{fv}(\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=\emptyset)
.

We provide a binding-free codatatype for local trees, whose structure derives from their syntax.

Definition A.12 (Semantic local trees).

Semantic local trees
(datatype
rl_ty
in

Local/Tree.v
), ranged over by
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, are terms generated
coinductively
by the following grammar:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}&::=&\\
&&\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\mathtt{end}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\mathbin{\mathbf{|\!\!|}}\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\mathbin{\mathbf{|\!\!|}}\\
&&\;\mathbin{\mathbf{|\!\!|}}\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\end{array}

We require that
{\sf p}\neq{\sf q}
,
I\neq\emptyset,
and
\ell_{i}\neq\ell_{j}
whenever
i\neq j,
for all
i,j\in I
.

As is done for global types, we define the unravelling of a local type
into a local tree.

Definition A.13 (Local unravelling).

Unravelling of local types
(definition
LUnroll
in

Local/Unravel.v
) is the relation between local types and semantic
trees coinductively specified by the following rules:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{l-unr-end}]}}&\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{l-unr-rec}]}}\\
\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\dfrac{}{\phantom{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\mathtt{end}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}$\cr\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\dfrac{\phantom{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\mathtt{end}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}$}\crcr}}&\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\dfrac{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}/\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\phantom{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}$\cr\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\dfrac{\phantom{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}/\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}$}\crcr}}\\[8.53581pt]
\lx@intercol\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{l-unr-send}]}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
\lx@intercol\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\dfrac{\forall i\in I.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\phantom{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{![{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}$\cr\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\dfrac{\phantom{\forall i\in I.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{![{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}$}\crcr}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[8.53581pt]
\lx@intercol\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{l-unr-recv}]}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\
\lx@intercol\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\dfrac{\forall i\in I.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\phantom{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}$\cr\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\dfrac{\phantom{\forall i\in I.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}$}\crcr}}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

Remark A.14.

We have required several “well-formedness” properties to types.

(1)

I\neq\emptyset
in Definitions
A.1
,

A.4
,
A.9
and

A.12
, namely the continuations for global/local
types/trees are not allowed to be empty.

(2)

Every recursion constructor in global/local types must be
guarded (Definitions
A.2
and
A.10
).

(3)

We only consider closed global/local types (Definitions

A.3
and
A.11
).

In the rest of the paper we continue to implicitly assume those for
each object we consider; however in the Coq development such
conditions must be made explicit in definitions and statements. We
have formalised them with (co)inductive predicates. In particular for
global types we have defined
g_precond
(in
Global/Syntax.v
)
exactly as the conjunction of the three predicates listed above, while
for global trees we have defined
WF
(in
Projection/CProject.v
) to
ensure that the continuations in the tree are never empty.

A.3.
Projections, or how to discipline communication

At the very core of the theory of multiparty session types, there is
the notion of
projection
. We have laid down a setting, where
global types offer a bird’s-eye perspective on communication and local
types take instead the point of view of a single participant. The
following definition is formalised to make sure that participants
respect what is globally prescribed for the protocol: each local type

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
protocol must be the projection, onto the respective
participant, of the global type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

Definition A.15.

The
projection of a global type onto a participant
{\sf r}
(
project
in

Projection/IProject.v
) is a partial function

{\_}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}:\texttt{g\_ty}\nrightarrow\texttt{l\_ty}

defined by recursion on
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
whenever the recursive call is defined:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{proj-end}]}}&\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{proj-var}]}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{end}}&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\\[5.69054pt]
\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{proj-rec}]}}&\\
\lx@intercol{(\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}.\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}})\ \text{if}\ \texttt{guarded}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}})\hfil\lx@intercol\\[5.69054pt]
\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{proj-send}]}}&\\
\lx@intercol{\sf r}={\sf p}\ \text{implies}\ {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{![{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}}\}_{i\in I}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[5.69054pt]
\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{proj-recv}]}}&\\
\lx@intercol{\sf r}={\sf q}\ \text{implies}\ {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}}\}_{i\in I}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[5.69054pt]
\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{proj-cont}]}}&\\
\lx@intercol\begin{array}[]{l}\text{${\sf r}\neq{\sf p}$, ${\sf r}\neq{\sf q}$ and $\forall i,j\in I$, ${\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}$; implies}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}\end{array}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[11.38109pt]
\lx@intercol\text{undefined if none of the above applies.}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

We describe the clauses of Definition
A.15
:

\DefTirNameOld
[proj-end,proj-var]
:

give the projections for end-types
and type variables;

\DefTirNameOld
[proj-rec]
:

gives the projection
on recursive types;

\DefTirNameOld
[proj-send]
(resp.
\DefTirNameOld
[proj-recv]
):

states that a global type starting with a communication from
{\sf r}
to
{\sf q}
(resp. from
{\sf q}
to
{\sf r}
) projects onto a sending (resp. receiving) local type

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{![{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(resp.
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}_{i}).{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
), provided that the continuations

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}
are also projections of the corresponding
global type continuations
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
;

\DefTirNameOld
[proj-cont]
:

states that, if the projected global type
starts with a communication between
{\sf p}
and
{\sf q}
and if we are
projecting it onto a third participant
{\sf r}
, then, for the
projection to be defined, we need to make sure that continuation is

the same
on all branches.

To prove the main result of trace equivalence in Coq, we want to
conveniently work with coinductive trees, hence we also define the
projection of a global tree onto a participant.

Example A.16 (Projection).

We show the examples of projection of global types.
First we would not be able to project

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{{\sf Alice}}\to{{\sf Bob}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\{\ell_{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{{\sf Bob}}\to{{\sf Carol}}:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5},}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\ell_{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{{\sf Alice}}\to{{\sf Carol}}:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

onto
Carol
, since, after skipping the first interaction between

Alice
and
Bob
, it would not be clear whether
Carol
should
expect a message
from
Alice
or from
Bob
. If instead we have

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{{\sf Alice}}\to{{\sf Bob}}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\{\ell_{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{{\sf Bob}}\to{{\sf Carol}}:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5},\ell_{2}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{bool}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{{\sf Bob}}\to{{\sf Carol}}:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

the projection
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{{\sf Carol}}}
is well defined as the local type

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{{\sf Bob}}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{end}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. In Coq we have rendered
this behaviour encoding the projection codomain as

option

l_ty
, as is common practice when
formalising partial functions.

Definition A.17.

The
projection of a coinductive global tree onto a participant
{\sf r}
(definitions
Project

and
IProj
in

Projection/CProject.v
) is a relation

\_\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \_\ :\ \texttt{rel}\ \ {\texttt{g\_ty}^{\texttt{c}}}\ \ {\texttt{l\_ty}^{\texttt{c}}}
coinductively
specified by the following clauses:

\small\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{co-proj-end}]}}\\
\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\dfrac{\neg\ \texttt{part\_of}\ {{\sf r}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\phantom{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\mathtt{end}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}$\cr\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\dfrac{\phantom{\neg\ \texttt{part\_of}\ {{\sf r}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\mathtt{end}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}$}\crcr}}\\[11.38109pt]
\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{co-proj-send-1}]}}\\
\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\dfrac{{\sf r}={\sf p}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \forall i\in I.\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\phantom{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}$\cr\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\dfrac{\phantom{{\sf r}={\sf p}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \forall i\in I.\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}$}\crcr}}\\[11.38109pt]
\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{co-proj-send-2}]}}\\
\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\dfrac{{\sf r}\neq{\sf q}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \forall i\in I.\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}{\phantom{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\mathrel{\hbox to10.55pt{\vbox to12.98pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.27615pt\lower-7.88745pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

Definition A.18.

A role
{\sf p}
is said to be
participant of a global tree

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

(definition
part_of
in
Global/Tree.v
), when for
{\sf p}
and
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
the
following inductively defined predicate,
\texttt{part\_of}{}\ \_\ \_
, holds:

\small\begin{array}[]{l}\dfrac{}{\texttt{part\_of}\ {{\sf p}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\\[8.53581pt]
\dfrac{}{\texttt{part\_of}\ {{\sf q}}\ {\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\\[11.38109pt]

\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}\end{array}

Definition
A.18
captures the same concept as
prts

for global types. Such a predicate is inductive in its nature, even on
a coinductive datatype; the intuitive reason for this is that if
{\sf p}

is a participant of
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
it should be found, as a sending or
receiving role, within a
finite
, albeit arbitrary, number of
steps in the branching structure of
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. By factoring in the
predicate
part_of
, Definition
A.17
ensures (1) that the
projection of a global tree on a participant outside the protocol is

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\mathtt{end}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(rule
\DefTirNameOld
[co-proj-end]
) and (2) that this discipline
is preserved in the continuations (rule
\DefTirNameOld
[co-proj-cont]
).

From a formalisation point of view, if we had tried to define the
above tree projection as a corecursive function, instead as
coinductive relation, we would have incurred problems for rules

\DefTirNameOld
[co-proj-send-2]
and
\DefTirNameOld
[co-proj-cont]
: here, the
corecursive call of
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
does not appear guarded by any
constructor. Also in rule
\DefTirNameOld
[co-proj-cont]
, we would need to
provide a coinductive proof for the hypothesis

\forall i,j\in I.\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{j}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and this would
lead to further complications. On the other side, working with
projection as a relation is common practice in the literature (see,
e.g.,
(
Ghilezan et al. 2019
, Definition 3.6)
) and allowed us to have a
smoother development for trees in Coq.

Example A.19.

The projection of coinductive trees is slightly more permissive than
its inductive counterpart, as pointed out in
(
Ghilezan et al. 2019
)
,
Remark 3.14. Let us consider for example:

(1)

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\{\ \ell_{0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{0},\ell_{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{1}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

with

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}.\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf p}\to{\sf r}:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf p}\to{\sf r}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}.\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf p}\to{\sf r}:}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.
Then we have:

(2)

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\\
\neq\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mu\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}.\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{?[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}).\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}\ \text{,}\end{array}

then no rule from Definition
A.15
applies (in
particular

\DefTirNameOld
[proj-cont]
does not), and the projection onto

{\sf r}
for
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is undefined. On the other end, it is clear that

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is obtained by
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{G_{0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
with “one step of
unravelling” or, formally, that the infinite tree associated by
\Re

to both of
them is the same

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{01}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf r}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf r}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}).\;\dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Indeed we observe that

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf p}\to{\sf r}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, thus by
rule
\DefTirNameOld
[g-unr-rec]
of Definition
A.5
, we have
that from
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{1}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Re\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{01}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Re\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{01}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
must hold.
Thus we have:

(3)

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Re\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\text{with}\quad\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\{\ \ell_{0}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{01},\ell_{1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{01}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

By choosing

\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{01}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{nat}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).\ \dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we can indefinitely apply

\DefTirNameOld
[co-proj-recv-1]
to get

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{01}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{01}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and thus we
finally obtain
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
by
\DefTirNameOld
[co-proj-cont]
:
namely while
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
does not admit any projection on
{\sf r}
, its
unravelling
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
does.

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(M.1)
\Re
\Re
\upharpoonright
\upharpoonright^{\textsf{c}}

The above example shows that, inside a global type, when two branches
of a continuation are obtained by a different number of unravelling
steps of the same recursion type, syntactic projection (Definition

A.15
) gets stuck. At the same time its coinductive
counterpart (Definition
A.17
) handles smoothly this
case, thanks to infinite unravelling that gives such recursion global
types the same representation.

To conclude this subsection, we state our first main result from the
formalisation, namely that unravelling preserves projections. This
completes the first metatheory
square
(M.1)
of
the diagram
in Figure
2
.

Theorem A.20 (Unravelling preserves projections).

(Theorem
ic_proj
in
Projection/Correctness.v
.)

Given a global type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, such that
\texttt{guarded}\ \color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\texttt{closed}\ \color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, if

(a)

there exists a local type
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright}{{\sf r}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\
,

(b)

there exists a global tree
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and,

(c)

there exists a local tree
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,

then
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

Proof Outline.

The full proof is found in
Projection/Correctness.v
of our Coq formalisation. As an outline, before performing coinduction on the definition of the coinductive projection in
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, we rule out the case in which
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
has the shape
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}.\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. Formally this first step goes as follows.

•

Since
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}.\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}^{\prime}\{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}/\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}.\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}{}^{\prime}\}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
have the same unravelling
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(Definition
A.5
), we can perform such operation (
finite unravelling
, definition) on
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\mu\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{X}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}.\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
until we get
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
that is either a message-type or an end-type (we have as an hypothesis that
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is closed and closure is preserved by finite unravelling).

•

We have proved that if
{{\sf r}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
{{\sf r}}{\upharpoonright}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
then
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}{}_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\;\Re\;\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
(lemma
LUnroll_ind
in
Local/Unravel.v
).

Thus, proving the theorem for every non-recursion global type
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}{}_{n}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, gives us the theorem for every
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
global type. We therefore assume that
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\sf G}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is not a recursion-type and proceed by coinduction on
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf r}}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\sf L}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. We use the features of the PaCo
(
Hur
et al. 2013
)
to modularise the proof: in a separate Coq lemma we can assume the coinductive hypothesis in the context and prove the statement with guardedness guaranteed by the PaCo features (lemma
project_nonrec
in
Projection/Correctness.v
).
∎

A.4.
Projection Environments for Asynchronous Communication

In this subsection, we introduce key concepts for building an
asynchronous operational semantics for multiparty session types. We
define our semantics following
(
Deniélou and
Yoshida 2013
)
, where a
precise correspondence is drawn between communicating finite-state
automata and multiparty session types. We do not formalise an explicit
syntax for automata, but develop labelled transition systems for
global and local trees with automata in mind. We rely on
queue
environments
as communication buffers, shared between pairs of
local trees, which allow for asynchronicity of the execution, while
guaranteeing the disciplined behaviour of participants. Let us start
with a paradigmatic example: a simple message exchange between two
participants.

Example A.21 (Local trees for a simple message exchange).

Below we informally use the notation
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\xrightarrow{\text{step}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
to indicate
one semantic step between local trees.
{\sf p}
sends a message to
{\sf q}

with label
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and payload of sort
S
and continues on

\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, and dually
{\sf q}
receives from
{\sf p}
the message, with same
label and payload, and then continues on
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
:

\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xrightarrow{\text{step}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xrightarrow{\text{step}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

For
{\sf q}
to receive the message, it is necessary that
{\sf p}
has first
sent the message. To model this asynchronous behaviour, we use FIFO
queues: in the designated queue
Q({\sf p},{\sf q})
(empty at first) we
enqueue the message sent from
{\sf p}
and we store it, until the
message is received by
{\sf q}
and dequeued.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q({\sf p},{\sf q})=\texttt{empty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q({\sf p},{\sf q})=[(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q({\sf p},{\sf q})=\texttt{empty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{}^{\prime}}}
step
enqueue
dequeue
step

Figure 11
.
Simple message exchange with a FIFO queue as buffer.

Figure
11
summarises the intuition behind the semantics of a
simple message exchange, starting from an empty queue: the message
is received immediately after it has been sent. Such a FIFO queue
allows for storing more than one message sent from
{\sf p}
to
{\sf q}
,
which
{\sf q}
will receive in the same order they have been sent,
according to the first-in-first-out discipline.

Considering our global protocols, we need one queue for each ordered
pair of participants
({\sf p},{\sf q})
to store the messages sent from
{\sf p}
to

{\sf q}
. We formally collect such queues in
queue environments
.

Definition A.22 (Queue environments).

We call
queue environment
any finitely supported function that
maps a pair of participants into a finite sequence (
queue
) of
pairs of labels and sorts.

In Coq we write the above type as

\texttt{qenv}=\{\texttt{fmap}\ \ \ \texttt{role}*\texttt{role}\rightarrow\texttt{seq}\;(\texttt{label}*\texttt{mty})\}

(Notation
qenv
in
Local/Semantics.v
), where we have used
support from the Mathematical Components libraries

(
Gonthier and
Mahboubi 2010
)
for datatypes as finite function (
fmap
) and
lists (or
sequences
,
seq
). Finite maps are formalised as
partial functions
9
9

9

More detail can be found at the
Mathematical Components web page

https://math-comp.github.io/
.
with additional structure for
their finite domain. We use the Coq constructor
None
for the
default return value of a partial function applied to an input value
outside its domain.

On queue environments we have defined the operation of enqueuing

enq
and dequeuing
deq
as:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\texttt{enq}\ Q\ ({\sf p},{\sf q})\ (\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})&=&Q[({\sf p},{\sf q})\mapsfrom Q({\sf p},{\sf q})@(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})]\\[2.84526pt]
\texttt{deq}\ Q\ ({\sf p},{\sf q})&=&\texttt{if}\ Q({\sf p},{\sf q})=(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\#s\\
&&\texttt{then}\ ((\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}),Q[({\sf p},{\sf q})\mapsfrom s])\\
&&\texttt{else}\ \texttt{None}\end{array}

As for notation, we use
\#
as the “cons” constructor for lists and

@
as the “append” operation;
f[x\mapsfrom y]
denotes the
updating of a function
f
in
x
with
y
, namely

f[x\mapsfrom y]\ x^{\prime}=f\ x^{\prime}
for all
x\neq x^{\prime}
and

f[x\mapsfrom y]\ x=y
, and
None
is the default value for
partial functions provided by Coq. In case the sequence
Q({\sf p},{\sf q})
is
empty
deq
will not perform any operation on it, but return

None
; in case the sequence is not empty it will return both
its head and its tail (as a pair). We denote the empty queue
environment by
\epsilon
, namely
\epsilon\ ({\sf p},{\sf q})=\texttt{None}
for
all
({\sf p},{\sf q})
.

Queue environments are used to regulate the asynchronous message
passing among participants for the whole protocol. We adapt the
projection of global types onto queue environment from
(
Deniélou and
Yoshida 2013
, Appendix
A.1)
, to our coinductive setting.

Definition A.23 (Queue projection).

(Definition
qProject
in

Projection/QProject.v
)
The
projection on queue environments
of a global tree
(
queue projection
for short) is the relation
{\_}{\upharpoonright^{\texttt{q}}}{\_}\ :\ \texttt{rel}\ \ {\texttt{g\_ty}^{\texttt{c}}}\ \ {\texttt{qenv}}
coinductively specified by the following clauses:

{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.6 -7.98 L 2.62 -7.98 L 1.44 -8.95 L -0.92 -7.01 L -3.28 -8.95 L -5.65 -7.01 L -8.16 -7.98 L -8.16 -7.98}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{5.69432pt}{-5.76942pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
has
Q^{\prime}
as its
projections, if
\texttt{deq}\ Q^{\prime}({\sf p},{\sf q})=((\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j}),Q)
and if
Q
is
projection for each continuation
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.

Remark A.24.

For the sake of on-paper
presentation, the above definition is presented as a coinductive
predicate, dealing with coinductive objects (trees). Albeit this
definition carries the correct concept, formally it is not accurate:
in our formalisation,
qProject
is defined in Coq not as a
codatatype, but as a datatype,
inductively
on prefixes for
global trees (see Remark
A.6
).

Queue projection has allowed us to associate to a global tree, in one
shot, all the queues involved in the protocol collected in a queue
environment. Along the same lines, we will consider all the local
types of the protocol at once, by defining the type of
local
environments
.

Definition A.25 (Local environments).

We call
local environment
, or simply
environment
, any
finitely supported function
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
that maps participants into
local types.

In Coq we write the above type as

\texttt{renv}=\{\texttt{fmap}\ \ \ \texttt{role}\rightarrow\texttt{rl\_ty}\}
(Notation
renv
in

Local/Semantics.v
).

As anticipated, we are interested in those environments that are
defined on the participants of a protocol (global tree
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
) and
that map each participant
{\sf p}
to the projection of
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
onto such

{\sf p}
.

Definition A.26 (Environment projection).

(Definition

eProject
in
Projection/CProject.v
.)

We say that
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is an environment projection for
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
notation
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\upharpoonright\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, if it holds that

\forall{\sf p}.\ \color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ {\upharpoonright^{\texttt{c}}}{{\sf p}}\ (\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E\ {\sf p}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
.

In the next sections we establish a relation between the semantics for
global and local types. In the local case, we define the semantics on
local environments together with queue environments. In the statements
of our soundness and completeness results we therefore consider the
projection of a global tree both on local environments and on queue
environments, together in one shot.

Definition A.27 (One-shot projection).

(Definition

Projection
in
Projection.v
)

We say that the pair of a local environment and of a queue environment
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)
is a (one-shot) projection for the global tree
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, notation
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\upharpoonright\!\upharpoonright(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)
if it holds that:

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\upharpoonright\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad\text{and}\quad{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{\upharpoonright^{\texttt{q}}}{Q}
.

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf q}\to{\sf p}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf q}\to{\sf p}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\allowbreak\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}).\dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \
. Participant
{\sf p}
has sent a message to
{\sf q}
,
{\sf q}
will receive it
next (but has not yet) and then the protocol continues indefinitely
with
{\sf q}
sending a message to
{\sf p}
after the other. We define

\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
—with support
\{{\sf p},{\sf q}\}
, since these are the only two
participants involved—such that:

\ \ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E\ {\sf p}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).\dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \
and

\ \ \color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E\ {\sf q}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\ell(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}).\dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \
. We then
define
Q
—with support a subset of
\{({\sf p},{\sf q}),({\sf q},{\sf p})\}
, since
messages are sent only from
{\sf p}
to
{\sf q}
or from
{\sf q}
to
{\sf p}
—such
that:
\ \ Q({\sf p},{\sf q})=[(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})]\ \
and

\ \ Q({\sf q},{\sf p})=\texttt{None}
. Following the definitions in this
section it is easy to verify that
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\upharpoonright\!\upharpoonright(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)
; observe
that the only “message” enqueued in
Q
is
(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, since
this is the only one sent, but not yet received (at this stage of
the execution).

A.5.
Labelled Transition Relations for Tree Types

We define
trace semantics
both for types and for processes. At
the core of trace semantics that we define for session types, lies a
labelled transition system (LTS) defined on trees, with regard to

actions
. In this section we present the basic definitions and
results—up to soundness and completeness of the local reduction with
respect to the global one—, following the structure of our Coq
formalisation.

The basic
actions
(datatype
act
in
Common/Actions.v
) of
our asynchronous communication are objects, ranged over by
a
, of the
shape either:

•

!{\sf p}{\sf q}(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
: send
!
action, from participant
{\sf p}
to
participant
{\sf q}
, of label
\ell
and payload type
S
, or

•

?{\sf q}{\sf p}(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
: receive
?
action, from participant
{\sf p}
at
participant
{\sf q}
, of label
\ell
and payload type
S
.

We define the
subject
of an action
a
(definition

subject
in
Common/Actions.v
),
\texttt{subj}\ {a}
, as
{\sf p}
if

a=!{\sf p}{\sf q}(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
and as
{\sf q}
if
a=?{\sf q}{\sf p}(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
.
10
10

10

The representation of actions is directly taken from

(
Deniélou and
Yoshida 2013
)
, however we have swapped the order of

{\sf p}
and
{\sf q}
in the receive action, so that the subject of an
action always occurs in first position.

Given an action, our types (represented as trees) can perform a
reduction step.

Definition A.29 (LTS for global trees (
step
in
Global/Semantics.v
)).

The
labelled transition relation for global trees

(
global reduction
or
global step
for short) is, for each action
a
, the relation

\_\ \xrightarrow{a}\ \_\ :\ \texttt{rel}\ \ {\texttt{g\_ty}^{\texttt{c}}}\ \ {\texttt{g\_ty}^{\texttt{c}}}
inductively specified
by the following clauses:

\DefTirNameOld
[g-step-str1]
:

with an action
a
a step is allowed
to be performed under a sending constructor

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\dots}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
each time that the subject of that
action is different from
{\sf p}
and from
{\sf q}
and, coinductively, each
continuation steps accordingly, namely

\forall i\in I.\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\xrightarrow{a}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}^{\prime}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
;

, and each other
continuation stays the same, namely

\forall i\in I\backslash\{j\}.\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{i}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}^{\prime}_{i}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
.

Remark A.30.

The semantics allows for some degree of non-determinism. For instance,

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
could perform a step according to both
rules
\DefTirNameOld
[g-step-send]
and
\DefTirNameOld
[g-step-str1]
(depending
on the subject of the action).

Below we formalise the intuition from Example
A.21
, and we
define a transition system for environments of local trees, together
with environments of queues.

Definition A.31 (LTS for environments (
l_step
in
Local/Semantics.v
)).

The
labelled transition relation for environments

(
local reduction
or
local step
for short) is, for each
action
a
, the relation

\_\ \xrightarrow{a}\ \_\ :\ \texttt{rel}\ \ {(\texttt{renv}*\texttt{qenv})}\ \ {(\texttt{renv}*\texttt{qenv})}

inductively specified by the following clauses:

\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{l-step-send}]}}\\
\dfrac{a=!{\sf p}{\sf q}(\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j})\quad\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E\ {\sf p}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}{(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)\xrightarrow{a}(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E[{\sf p}\mapsfrom\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{j}]}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\texttt{enq}\ Q\ ({\sf p},{\sf q})\ (\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j}))}\\[14.22636pt]
\color[rgb]{0.2813,0.2383,0.5469}{\DefTirNameOld{\footnotesize[{l-step-recv}]}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\qquad a=?{\sf q}{\sf p}(\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j})\\
\dfrac{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E\ {\sf q}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\quad Q({\sf p},{\sf q})=(\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j})\#s}{(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)\xrightarrow{a}(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E[{\sf q}\mapsfrom\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{j}]}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q[({\sf p},{\sf q})\mapsfrom s])}\end{array}

Notice that, if the third condition in the premise of

\DefTirNameOld
[l-step-recv]
is satisfied, in its conclusion

Q[({\sf p},{\sf q})\mapsfrom s]=\pi_{2}\ (\texttt{deq}\ Q\ ({\sf p},{\sf q}))
(where
\pi_{2}
is
the projection on the second component of a pair).

Basic send/receive steps for global and local trees

Example A.32 (Basic send/receive steps for global and local trees).

Figure
12(a)
shows the transitions for a global tree,
regulating the sending of a message from
{\sf p}
to
{\sf q}
, and
simultaneously the local transition for its projection on
{\sf p}
. The
asynchronicity of our system is witnessed by the two different
steps:
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(g.1)}
, for the sending action

!{\sf p}{\sf q}(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
, and
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(g.2)}
, for the receiving one

?{\sf q}{\sf p}(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
. Projecting

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\to{\sf q}:}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{(\ell,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}).}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
on
{\sf p}
(arrow

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(g.1)}
) gives us a local tree that performs a sending
step
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(l.1)}
corresponding to
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(g.1)}
, and
projection is preserved (arrow
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(p.2)}
). However this
does not happen for the receiving step
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(g.2)}
: here
the projections on
{\sf p}
of

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf p}\mathrel{\hbox to8.45pt{\vbox to11.97pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.2264pt\lower-7.38329pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.67363pt}{-2.43054pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}

{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}}{{}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}

along

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(p.2)}
and of
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
along
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(p.3)}
are
the same. The situation is dual if we consider the projection on the
receiving participant
{\sf q}
, Figure
4(b)
. Here the
projections along
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(q.1)}
and
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(q.2)}
,
corresponding to the global tree performing a sending action, result
in the same local tree. We have instead a local step

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(l.2)}
preserving the local projections on
{\sf q}
along

\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(q.2)}
and
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(q.3)}
for the receiving
action along
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{(g.2)}
.

Figure
12
confirms our intuition: when the global tree
performs one step,
there is one local tree
, projection of the
global tree on a participant, such that it performs a corresponding
step. We have indeed defined semantics for collections of local trees,
as opposed to single local trees. The formal relation of the
small-step reductions with respect to projection is established with
soundness and completeness results.

Theorem A.33 (Step Soundness).

(Theorem
Project_step
in
TraceEquiv.v
)

If
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xrightarrow{a}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\upharpoonright\!\upharpoonright(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)
,
there exist
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
and
Q^{\prime}
such that

\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\upharpoonright\!\upharpoonright(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
and

(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)\xrightarrow{a}(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
.

Proof Outline.

The proof follows the intuition displayed by Figure
12
. We identify three major proof steps:

(1)

we explicitly build the pair
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
from
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)
;

(2)

we prove
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\upharpoonright\!\upharpoonright(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
;

(3)

we prove
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)\xrightarrow{a}(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
.

(1)
The pair
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
is the result of the function
run_step
in
Local/Semantics.v
, applied to
a
and
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)
. It is defined as follows:

•

if
a=!{\sf p}{\sf q}(\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j})
and
\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E\ {\sf p}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{!^{\sf c}[{{\sf q}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
, then
\texttt{run\_step}\ a\ (\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)=(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
, where
E^{\prime}=E[{\sf p}\mapsfrom\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{j}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]
and
Q^{\prime}=\texttt{enq}\ Q\ ({\sf p},{\sf q})\ (\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j})
;

•

if
a=?{\sf q}{\sf p}(\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j})
,

\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E\ {\sf p}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{?^{\sf c}[{{\sf p}}];\{\ell_{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}}_{i}).\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{i}\}_{i\in I}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

and
Q({\sf p},{\sf q})=(\ell_{j},\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\texttt{S}}{}_{j})\#s
,
then
\texttt{run\_step}\ a\ (\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)=(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
, where
E^{\prime}=E[{\sf q}\mapsfrom\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{{\sf L^{c}}}{}_{j}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]
and
Q^{\prime}=\pi_{2}(\texttt{deq}\ Q\ ({\sf p},{\sf q}))
;

•

if none of the above
\texttt{run\_step}\ a\ (\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)=(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)
(this is just intended as a default output to formally define the function in Coq).

Note that we have built
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
according to the effect that we expect that the one-step local reduction has on
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q)
.

(2)
In order to prove that our candidate
(\color[rgb]{0.332,0.418,0.1836}{E^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0},Q^{\prime})
is indeed projection for
G^{\prime}
. In the formalisation we have outsourced this to the lemma
runstep_proj
in
TraceEquiv.v
. The proof proceed by induction on the step relation (Definition
3.13
) in hypothesis
\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\xrightarrow{a}\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{\color[rgb]{0.0039,0.4727,0.4336}{{\sf G}^{\sf c}}{}^{\prime}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
. The base cases, corresponding to rules
\DefTirNameOld
[g-step-send]
and
\DefTirNameOld
[g-step-rcv]
, are handled by the two following lemmas (both in
TraceEquiv.v
):
